---
name: "pdf"
description: "Read, create, inspect, render, and verify PDF files where visual layout matters, including fillable AcroForms. Use Poppler rendering plus Python tools such as reportlab, pdfplumber, and pypdf for generation and extraction."
---

# PDF Skill

## When To Use

- Read or review PDF content where layout and visuals matter.
- Create PDFs programmatically with reliable formatting.
- Fill and validate interactive PDF forms.
- Validate final rendering before delivery.

## Tools + Contract Requirements

Immediately before the first create/edit authoring command, run `mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs` successfully exactly once using the command below. Do not run it for read-only work. For edits, replace `create` with `edit`; adjust the expected count and output format to match the requested outputs.



```bash
node container_tools/mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs --operation-kind create --expected-output-count 1 --output-format pdf
```

## Workflow

1. Prefer visual review: render PDF pages to PNGs and inspect them.
   - Use `pdftoppm` from the bundled runtime or system Poppler when available.
   - If unavailable, install Poppler or ask the user to review the output locally.
2. Use `reportlab` to generate PDFs when creating new documents.
3. Use `pdfplumber` or `pypdf` for text extraction and quick checks; do not rely on text extraction for layout fidelity.
4. After each meaningful update, re-render pages and verify alignment, spacing, and legibility.

## Fill And Validate AcroForms

Visual review alone is not a correctness check for a fillable PDF. A page `/Widget` annotation can render a value from its appearance stream while the canonical `/AcroForm/Fields` tree is missing or contains a stale value.

1. Keep the result interactive by default; set `flatten=True` only when the user explicitly requests a completed, static form. Preserve the source PDF, and do not flatten a signed PDF without an explicit workflow decision.
2. Inspect both representations before filling: enumerate fields from `reader.get_fields()` and `/Widget` annotations from every page's `/Annots`, following `/Parent` and `/Kids`. If a widget and a canonical field have the same name but are distinct objects with no `/Parent` relationship, do not call `reattach_fields()` blindly: it can create a second top-level field with the same name. Report the ambiguity or produce a static result.
3. Recover genuinely orphaned widgets, fill all pages, and write the result with `pypdf`:

```python
from pypdf import PdfReader, PdfWriter
````

from pathlib import Path
from html.parser import HTMLParser
import hashlib,json,shutil,subprocess,re,argparse
import pymupdf
web=Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
project=web.parent/'MiniProject';style=web/'templates/milestone_visual.tex'
parser=argparse.ArgumentParser()
parser.add_argument('--content',type=Path,default=web/'templates/milestone2_content.json')
parser.add_argument('--archive-only',action='store_true')
options=parser.parse_args()
data=json.loads(options.content.read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
version=data['version'];page_count=len(data['pages'])
build=web/('output/pdf/milestone2-v'+version.replace('.',''));build.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True)
pub=web/'public/miniproject/milestone-2'
def tex(s):
 return ''.join({'\\':r'\textbackslash{}','&':r'\&','%':r'\%','$':r'\$','#':r'\#','_':r'\_','{':r'\{','}':r'\}','~':r'\textasciitilde{}','^':r'\textasciicircum{}'}.get(c,c) for c in s)
class HTMLTex(HTMLParser):
 def __init__(self):super().__init__();self.parts=[]
 def handle_starttag(self,tag,attrs):
  attr=dict(attrs)
  if tag=='b':self.parts.append(r'\textbf{')
  elif tag=='font':self.parts.append(r'\texttt{')
  elif tag=='link':self.parts.append(r'\href{'+attr['href'].replace('%',r'\%').replace('#',r'\#')+'}{')
  elif tag=='br':self.parts.append(r'\par ')
 def handle_endtag(self,tag):
  if tag in ['b','font','link']:self.parts.append('}')
 def handle_data(self,s):self.parts.append(tex(s))
def html(s):p=HTMLTex();p.feed(s);return ''.join(p.parts)
def table(rows,widths):
 n=len(widths);ratios=[w/sum(widths)*n for w in widths]
 cols=''.join(r'>{\hsize='+f'{r:.5f}'+r'\hsize\raggedright\arraybackslash}X' for r in ratios)
 return '\n'.join([r'{\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.16}',r'\rowcolors{2}{PaleBlue}{white}',r'\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}'+cols+r'@{}}',r'\rowcolor{Blue!13} '+' & '.join(r'\textbf{'+html(c)+'}' for c in rows[0])+r' \\',*[' & '.join((r'\textbf{\color{Navy}'+html(c)+'}' if i==0 else html(c)) for i,c in enumerate(row))+r' \\' for row in rows[1:]],r'\end{tabularx}\par}',r'\vspace{1.7mm}'])

section=data.get('section_titles',['Dataset and predictive task','Previous learning solutions','Implementation and course connections','Contribution and evaluation','Discussion and references'])
section_numbers=data.get('section_numbers',list(range(1,page_count+1)))
assert len(section_numbers)==page_count
pdf_title=data.get('pdf_title','DS675 Mini-Project Milestone 2 - Working draft v'+version)
header_label=data.get('header_label','Draft v'+version)
parts=[r'\input{milestone_visual.tex}',r'\usepackage{graphicx}',r'\hypersetup{pdftitle={'+tex(pdf_title)+r'}}',r'\begin{document}\small']
for index,page in enumerate(data['pages']):
 if index:parts.append(r'\newpage')
 page_label=(tex(header_label)+' | ' if header_label else '')+str(index+1)+'/'+str(page_count)
 parts.append(r'\milestoneheader{2}{Learning Models for Pressure Forecasting}{'+page_label+'}')
 continuation=' (continued)' if index and section_numbers[index]==section_numbers[index-1] else ''
 parts.append(r'\sectiontitle{'+str(section_numbers[index])+'. '+section[index]+continuation+'}')
 for block in page:
  kind=block['kind'];args=block['args']
  if kind=='title':continue
  if kind=='p' and args[0].startswith('<b>DS675 Mini-Project'):continue
  if kind=='h':
   # Section name is now in the shared Navy section title.
   if re.match(r'^\d+\.\s',args[0]):continue
   parts.append(r'\vspace{1.5mm}\labeltext{'+html(args[0])+'}'+r'\par\vspace{.7mm}')
  elif kind=='table':parts.append(table(*args))
  elif kind=='image':
   shutil.copyfile(pub/args[0],build/args[0])
   parts.append(r'\begin{center}\includegraphics[width=\linewidth,height='+str(args[2])+r'in,keepaspectratio]{'+args[0]+r'}\end{center}{\footnotesize '+html(args[1])+r'\par}\vspace{1.8mm}')
  elif kind=='code':parts.append(r'\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=PaleBlue,colframe=Teal,boxrule=.4pt,arc=1mm,left=2mm,right=2mm,top=1mm,bottom=1mm]\footnotesize\begin{verbatim}'+'\n'+args[0]+'\n'+r'\end{verbatim}'+'\n'+r'\end{tcolorbox}')
  elif kind=='math':parts.append(r'\['+args[0]+r'\]')
  elif kind=='p':
   text=html(args[0])
   if args[0].startswith('<b>Draft status.'):
    parts.append(r'\begin{notebox}'+text+r'\end{notebox}\vspace{1mm}')
   elif section[index].startswith('Search') and args[0].startswith('Garðarsson et al. report'):
    parts.append(r'\begin{contextbox}'+text+r'\end{contextbox}')
   elif section[index].startswith('Prior work') and args[0].startswith('We propose to investigate'):
    parts.append(r'\begin{datasetbox}[Teal]{Candidate contribution}'+text+r'\end{datasetbox}\vspace{1mm}')
   elif index==page_count-1 and args[0].startswith('Before submission'):
    parts.append(r'\begin{notebox}'+text+r'\end{notebox}\vspace{1mm}')
   else:parts.append(text+r'\par\vspace{1.7mm}')
 if index==page_count-1:
  parts.append(r'\vspace{1mm}{\footnotesize')
  for label,url in data['references']:parts.append(r'\href{'+url+'}{'+tex(label)+r'}\par\vspace{1.4mm}')
  parts.append('}')
parts.append(r'\end{document}')
shutil.copyfile(style,build/'milestone_visual.tex')
source=build/'Milestone2-borrador.tex';source.write_text('\n'.join(parts),encoding='utf-8')
for _ in range(2):
 run=subprocess.run(['pdflatex','-interaction=nonstopmode','-halt-on-error',source.name],cwd=build,capture_output=True,text=True,encoding='utf-8',errors='replace',timeout=120)
 if run.returncode:
  print(run.stdout[-6500:]);raise SystemExit(run.returncode)
pdf=build/'Milestone2-borrador.pdf'
with pymupdf.open(pdf) as doc:
 assert len(doc)==page_count,f'Expected {page_count} pages, got {len(doc)}'
 assert all('Mini-Project Milestone 2' in p.get_text() for p in doc)
 for i,p in enumerate(doc):
  p.get_pixmap(matrix=pymupdf.Matrix(1.6,1.6)).save(build/f'page-{i+1}.png')
  if not options.archive_only:p.get_pixmap(matrix=pymupdf.Matrix(1.6,1.6)).save(pub/f'preview-{i+1}.png')
log=(build/'Milestone2-borrador.log').read_text(encoding='utf-8',errors='replace')
assert 'Overfull \\vbox' not in log,'Vertical overflow'
if not options.archive_only:
 shutil.copyfile(pdf,web/'output/pdf/Milestone2-borrador.pdf');shutil.copyfile(pdf,pub/'Milestone2-borrador.pdf')
local=project/('Milestone 2/entregables/borrador-v'+version);local.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True)
for p in [pdf,source,style]:shutil.copyfile(p,local/p.name)
for page in data['pages']:
 for block in page:
  if block['kind']=='image':shutil.copyfile(build/block['args'][0],local/block['args'][0])
shutil.copyfile(options.content,local/'milestone2_content.json')
master=project/'Plantilla';master.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True);shutil.copyfile(style,master/style.name)
provenance=json.loads((web/'docs/MILESTONE_PREVIEWS.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
for row in provenance['outputs']:
 if row['milestone']==2:row.update({'sha256':hashlib.sha256(pdf.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),'status':'working draft v'+version+'; same visual template as Milestone 1; not submitted'})
provenance['visual_template']={'master':'MiniProject/Milestone 1/Milestone1.tex','common_template':'templates/milestone_visual.tex','sha256':hashlib.sha256(style.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),'rules':'Latin Modern serif; Letter 10pt base; .64in horizontal/.42in vertical margins; Navy/Teal/Gold; shared header, striped tables and callout boxes.'}
if not options.archive_only:(web/'docs/MILESTONE_PREVIEWS.json').write_text(json.dumps(provenance,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')
print(json.dumps({'pages':page_count,'version':version,'sha256':hashlib.sha256(pdf.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),'overfull_hboxes':log.count('Overfull \\hbox'),'previous_draft_preserved':str(project/'Milestone 2/entregables/borrador-v0.2/Milestone2-borrador.pdf')}))

five=[
 h('3.1 Proposed extension and relation to previous work'),
 p('Can gradient-boosted regression trees forecast recorded n1 pressure at +15 min better than persistence and simple regression baselines? The principal temporal models reviewed use Chebyshev graph convolutions, adaptive kNN or gated/attention graph networks. We propose Gradient Boosting with lagged measurements from the six local signals in Section 1, using original SCADA rather than a new hydraulic simulation. Boosting already appears in L-Town leakage-region classifiers (Li-Chen/DHI China), and Artesia estimates pressure with ExtraTrees without a documented prospective lead. Our scoped review and additional targeted search did not identify this specific six-signal n1(+15) boosting experiment. The anticipated extension is a reproducible study of that task/model combination, not a new algorithm, first use of trees/boosting on L-Town or proof of worldwide priority [13,20,21].'),
 table([
 ['Planned contribution','Concrete output','How it extends the reviewed work'],
 ['C1. Forecast-ready data','A timestamped table linking past inputs to recorded n1 pressure 15 min later.','An explicit original-SCADA forecast task with a compact, hydraulically motivated input set.'],
 ['C2. Boosting for future pressure','Train a GradientBoostingRegressor for recorded n1(+15); compare with persistence, Ridge and Random Forest.','Uses a course-covered learner for a future-pressure target, distinct from the documented boosting classifiers.'],
 ['C3. Useful auxiliary signals','Compare six-signal and n1-only boosting; remove auxiliary groups in validation.','Tests whether tank, pump, peer pressures and local demand improve on n1 history alone.']]),
 h('3.2 Data preparation and supporting EDA'),
 p('Retain n1/n4/n31 pressure, T1 level, PUMP_1 flow and AMR_n1 demand. At each origin u, use readings at lags 0,5,...,55 min: 72 measurement columns plus sine/cosine of time of day and seven weekday indicators, for 81 inputs. Align timestamps, check units/spacing/missing values, and form y by shifting n1 three rows forward. Exclude incomplete windows and labels crossing a split boundary. Use only readings available by u; leak annotations are not predictors. Scale Ridge using training data only.'),
 p('The daily profiles, correlations and persistence errors in Section 1 provide supporting EDA. Strong persistence makes it essential to benchmark every learned forecast against current n1. Weak marginal pump correlation does not rule out usefulness jointly with T1. Before fitting, inspect training-period changes and residuals by time of day; correlations alone do not establish predictive benefit.'),
 h('3.3 Models linked to course material'),
 {'kind':'math','args':[r'\widehat p_{n1}(u+15)=F_M(X(u)),\qquad F_m(X)=F_{m-1}(X)+\eta h_m(X).']},
 p('With squared-error loss, each shallow regression tree h_m learns residuals y-F_(m-1)(X); learning rate eta scales its correction. The initial prediction is the training-target mean. These are training residuals within boosting, not a change of the pressure target or a leakage alarm. Module 11.6 and the Week 7 Gradient Boosting transcript cover this sequential error-correction idea. Ridge/Random Forest and validation/attribute selection provide familiar controls [22,23,24,25].')]

six=[
 h('3.4 Small controlled comparison'),
 table([
 ['Model','Inputs / training target','Purpose'],
 ['Persistence','Current n1; no fitting.','Essential operational baseline.'],
 ['Ridge reference','Six histories/calendar; future n1 pressure.','Regularized linear benchmark.'],
 ['Random Forest','Same inputs and future-pressure target.','Bagging/tree-ensemble benchmark.'],
 ['Gradient Boosting','Same inputs and future-pressure target.','Main proposed method: sequential boosted trees.'],
 ['Boosting, n1 only','n1 history/calendar (21 inputs); same target.','Checks the value of the five auxiliary signals.']]),
 p('Compare the learners on common origins and the same future-pressure target. Full versus n1-only boosting tests the auxiliary signals. As a validation-only feature check, remove T1, PUMP_1, the n4/n31 pair, or AMR_n1 from full boosting one group at a time. Use the same history and tuning rule. This class-based attribute-removal test measures predictive association, not causation [24].'),
 p('For boosting, cross 100/200/300 stages with learning rates 0.03/0.1; fix tree depth 2, minimum leaf size 20, squared-error loss, subsample=1 and seed 42. Disable automatic random-split early stopping. For Random Forest, use 200 trees, max_features=1.0, seed 42 and depth {6,10,14} crossed with minimum leaf size {10,40}. Ridge uses alpha {0.01,0.1,1,10,100,1000}. Each learner/input set has six candidate settings, selected by chronological validation MAE. These are project choices, not achieved results [22,23,25].'),
 h('3.5 Chronological evaluation and success criteria'),
 p('Train on January-September 2018, choose settings on October-December 2018, then refit on eligible 2018 origins and freeze models/settings before the new 2019 assessment. Use a common 55-minute history warm-up and remove origins whose +15 label crosses a partition boundary; earlier predictor history may cross it. Compare every final model on the same eligible origins. Since this public dataset has already been inspected, 2019 is a final temporal assessment, not an untouched blind test.'),
 {'kind':'math','args':[r'\mathrm{MAE}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_i|y_i-\hat y_i|,\quad \mathrm{RMSE}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_i(y_i-\hat y_i)^2},\quad \mathrm{skill}=1-\frac{\mathrm{MAE}_{model}}{\mathrm{MAE}_{persistence}}.']},
 p('Report MAE (primary) and RMSE in metres, plus skill relative to persistence. A boosting accuracy advantage requires lower final MAE than persistence, Ridge and Random Forest on common origins. Separately, call auxiliary signals useful if full boosting beats n1-only boosting and persistence. Report absolute/relative MAE differences and monthly errors to show whether an overall advantage is consistent. Disclose RMSE tradeoffs and null/negative results; these descriptive comparisons do not establish statistical significance.'),
 h('3.6 Feasible scope and anticipated deliverables'),
 p('Deliver the preparation notebook, fixed feature lists/split/settings, a model-comparison table and predicted-versus-observed/error plots. If boosting or auxiliary signals do not help, report the simpler successful baseline and the limitation. The scope uses familiar regression, ensembles and attribute selection; simulated communication delays, measurement-age features, storage-balance proxies and block-bootstrap inference are deferred. No learning model has been fitted and no accuracy gain is claimed for this report [21,24].')]

Vrachimis^{1} L8:   * cite2†Demetrios G. Eliades^{1} L9:   * cite3†Riccardo Taormina^{2} L10:   * cite4†Avi Ostfeld^{3} L11:   * cite5†Zoran Kapelan^{2} L12:   * cite6†Shuming Liu^{4} L13:   * cite7†Marios S. Kyriakou^{1} L14:   * cite8†Pavlos Pavlou^{1} L15:   * cite9†Mengning Qiu^{3} L16:   * cite10†Marios Polycarpou^{1} L17: \nL18: [Button: Show affiliations ]\nL19: \nL20:   * 1. KIOS Research and Innovation Center of Excellence\nL21:   * 2. Delft University of Technology\nL22:   * 3. Technion – Israel Institute of Technology\nL23:   * 4. Tsinghua University\nL24: ## Description\nL25: Drinking Water Distribution Networks (DWDN) are susceptible to infrastructure failures, which may lead to water losses. Typically, these water losses are due to background leakages and pipe bursts which may occur anywhere within the distribution network. Background leakages are normally difficult to detect due to their small size, whereas pipe bursts are easier to locate as they are of larger size and may appear on the surface.\nL26: The early detection and localization of some leakage event is extremely important, as this would reduce the time required for accommodating the event and therefore reducing the risk of further infrastructure degradation, contamination events and consumer complaints.\nL27: In previous years, a number of methodologies have been proposed to detect and isolate the location of leakage events using various types of sensor measurements. These methods were commonly evaluated on private commercial datasets, and as a result, it is not possible to objectively compare these methods in their ability to detect and isolate leaks.\nL28: In the past year, a leakage detection dataset has been proposed, LeakDB, based on benchmark networks and created using the WNTR tool, using pressure-driven demands and realistic leakage modelling. Inspired by the “BATtle of the Attack Detection ALgorithms” (BATADAL), which focused on the detection of cyber-physical attacks, our team decided to organize a similar “battle” focusing on leakage events.\nL29: The Battle of the Leakage Detection and Isolation Methods (BattLeDIM),  aims at objectively comparing the performance of methods for the detection and localization of leakage events, relying on SCADA measurements of flow and pressure sensors installed within water distribution networks. Participants may use different types of tools and methods, including (but not limited to) engineering judgement, machine learning, statistical methods, signal processing, and model-based fault diagnosis approaches.\nL30: ## Files\nL31: \nL32: ###\nL33: \nL34: 2018_Fixed_Leakages_Report.txt\nL35: \nL36: cite11†Preview L37: ###\nL38: \nL39: Files (550.5 MB)\nL40: Name  | Size  |\nL41: ---
